7
Related Work and Conclusion

Time in Session Types

Bocchi et al.
[
6
]
propose a timed extension of
MPST
to model real-time choreographic interactions, while
Bocchi et al.
[
5
]
extend
binary
session types with time constraints, introducing
a subtyping relation and a blocking receive primitive with timeout in their calculus. In contrast to their strategies to avoid time-related failures, as discussed in
§​

1
and
2
,
ATMP
focuses on actively managing failures as they occur, offering a distinct approach to handling timed communication.

Iraci et al.
[
25
]

extend
synchronous binary
session types
with a periodic recursion primitive to model rate-based processes.
To align their design with real-time systems, they encode time into a periodic construct, synchronised with a global clock. With
rate compatibility
,
a relation that facilitates communication between processes with different periods by
synthesising and verifying a common superperiod type, their approach ensures that well-typed processes
remain free from rate errors during any specific period.
On the contrary,
ATMP
integrates time constraints directly into communication through local clocks,
resulting in distinct time behaviour. Intriguingly, our method of time encoding can adapt to theirs,
while the opposite is not feasible.
Consequently, not all the timed protocols in our paper, e.g.
Fig.

1(b)
, can be accurately
represented in their system. Moreover, due to its
binary
and
synchronous
features,
their theory does not directly model and ensure the properties of real distributed systems.

Le Brun et al.
[
34
]
develop a theory of multiparty
session types that accounts for different failure scenarios, including message losses, delays, reordering,
as well as link failures and network partitioning.
Unlike
ATMP
, their approach does not integrate time specifications
or address failures specifically related to time.
Instead,
they use
timeout
as a generic message label for failure branches,
which triggers the failure detection mechanism.
For example, in
[
34
]
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}]}{\scriptsize\textifclk{\char 155\relax}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}
denotes a timeout branch, where
is the timeout notation representing
a non-deterministic time duration that a process waits before assuming a failure has arisen.
Except for
[
25
]
, all the mentioned works
on session types with time are purely theoretical.

Affinity, Exceptions and Error-Handling in Session Types

Mostrous and Vasconcelos
[
35
]
propose affine binary session
types with explicit cancellation,
which Fowler et al.
[
18
]

extend
to define Exceptional GV for binary asynchronous communication.
Exceptions can be
nested and handled over multiple communication actions,
and their implementation is an
extension of the research language
Links
. Harvey et al.
[
19
]
incorporate
MPST
with explicit connection actions to facilitate multiparty distributed communication,
and develop a code generator based on the actor-like research language
Ensemble
to implement their approach.
The work in
[
35
]

remains theoretical,
and both
[
35
,
18
]
are
limited to binary and linear logic-based session types. Additionally,
none of these works considers time specifications or
addresses the handling of time-related exceptions in their systems, which are key aspects of our work.

Session Types Implementations

MultiCrusty
,
extensively compared to
\texttt{MultiCrusty}^{T}
​ in this paper,
is a
Rust
implementation
based on affine
MPST
by Lagaillardie et al.
[
32
]
.
Their approach relies on
synchronous
communication, rendering
time and timeout exceptions unnecessary.

Cutner et al.
[
12
]

introduce
Rumpsteak
,
a
Rust
implementation
based on the
tokio

Rust
library,
which uses a different design
for asynchronous multiparty communications
compared to
\texttt{MultiCrusty}^{T}
​,
relying on the
crossbeam_channel

Rust
library.
The main goal of
[
12
]

is to compare the performance
of
Rumpsteak
,
mainly designed to analyse
asynchronous
message reordering,
to existing tools
such as the
k
-MC tool developed by Lange and Yoshida
[
33
]
.
Unlike
\texttt{MultiCrusty}^{T}
​,

Rumpsteak
lacks formalisation, or handling of timed communications and failures.

Typestate
is a
Rust
library implemented
by Duarte and Ravara
[
15
]
,
focused on helping
developers to write safer
APIs using typestates
and
their macros
#[typestate]
,

#[automaton]

and
#[state]
.

\texttt{MultiCrusty}^{T}
​ and
Typestate
are fundamentally different,
with
Typestate
creating a state machine
for checking possible errors in APIs
and not handling affine or timed communications.
Ferrite
,
a
Rust
implementation introduced by Chen et al.
[
8
]
,
is limited to binary session types
and forces the use of linear channels.
The modelling of

Ferrite
is based on
the shared binary session type
calculus
SILL
s
.

Jespersen et al.
[
26
]
and Kokke
[
29
]
propose

Rust
implementations
of binary session types
for synchronous communication
protocols.

[
25
]
extends the framework from
[
26
]

to encode the
rate compatibility
relation as a
Rust
trait and check whether two types are rate compatible.
Their approach is demonstrated with examples from rate-based systems,
including
[
2
,
39
,
48
]
.
Motivated by these applications, we formalise and
implement the respective timed protocols in
\texttt{MultiCrusty}^{T}
​,
showcasing the expressivity and feasibility of our system in real-time scenarios.

Neykova et al.
[
36
]
propose a
Python
runtime monitoring framework to handle timed
multiparty communications, guided by
[
6
]
.
They use a timed extension of
Scribble

[
49
]
to check the wait-freedom of protocols. The
Python
toolchain then

dynamically
checks that
the implementation
has no time violation or
communication mismatch.
However,
their verifications are time-consuming,
and may lead to valid messages
being rejected
[
36
, Fig. 16(b)]
.
Conversely,

\texttt{MultiCrusty}^{T}
​
statically
enforces
that each time window is reached
by a clock,
allowing every action to be triggered.
Moreover, through
Rust
’s affine types and meshed channels,
\texttt{MultiCrusty}^{T}
​ guarantees
linear usage of channels
and label matching by compilation.

Conclusion and Future Work

To address time constraints and timeout exceptions
in asynchronous communication,
we propose
affine timed multiparty session types
(
ATMP
)
along with the toolchain
\texttt{MultiCrusty}^{T}
​,
an implementation of
ATMP
in
Rust
.
Thanks to the incorporation of affinity and failure handling mechanisms,
our approach renders impractical conditions such as
wait-freedom
and
urgent receive
obsolete
while ensuring communication safety, protocol conformance, and deadlock-freedom, even in the presence of (timeout) failures.
Compared to
a synchronous toolchain without time,

\texttt{MultiCrusty}^{T}
​ exhibits negligible overhead
in various complex examples including those from real-time systems,
while enabling the verification of time constraints under asynchronous communication. As future work, we plan to
explore automatic recovery from errors
and timeouts instead of simply terminating
processes, which will involve extending the analysis of communication causality to timed global types and incorporating reversibility mechanisms into our system.

Appendix A
Proofs for Association

A.1
Unfoldings

We define the
unfolding
of timed global types as:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}\;=\;\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{\hskip 2mm}r@{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}}}\right\}}}}\right)}\hfil\hskip 5.69054pt&\text{if\, ${\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}}$}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\hfil\hskip 5.69054pt&\text{otherwise}\end{array}\right.\\
\end{array}

A recursive type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}
must be guarded (or
contractive), i.e. the unfolding leads to a progressive prefix, e.g. a
transmission.
Unguarded types, such as
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}^{\prime}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}}}}
, are not allowed.
Timed local types are subject to the similar definitions and requirements.

Note that any timed global type (or timed local type) we mention in this paper is closed and well-guarded.

Lemma 30
.

For a closed, well-guarded timed global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}
can only be of
form
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{\cdots}}}}\right\}}}
, or
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}{\rightsquigarrow}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}{:}{}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{\cdots}}}}\right\}}}
.
For a closed, well-guarded timed local type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}
can only be
of form
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
,
\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{\cdots\right\}}
, or

\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{\cdots\right\}}
.

Proof.

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}
will not appear since we require closed types.

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}^{\prime}}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\nicefrac{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}^{\prime}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}}}\right\}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}^{\prime}}}
since we require well-guarded types (recursive
types are contractive).
Similar argument for timed local types.
∎

Lemma 31
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright}\,\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}}{\upharpoonright}\,\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}
.

Proof.

By the definition of projection (
Def.

7
),
the definitions of unfolding recursive timed global and local types,
and
Lemma

30
.
∎

A.2
Subtyping

Lemma 32

(Subtyping is Reflexive)
.

For any closed, well-guarded timed local type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
holds.

Proof.

We construct a relation
R=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T})\right\}}}
.
It is trivial to show that
R
satisfies all clauses
of
Def.

6
, and hence,
R\subseteq\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}
.
∎

Lemma 33

(Subtyping is Transitive)
.

For any closed, well-guarded timed local type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{2}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{3}}
,
if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{2}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{2}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{3}}
hold, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{3}}

holds.

Proof.

By constructing a relation

R=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{3}})}\,\middle|\,{\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{2}}\text{ such that }{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{2}}\text{ and }{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{2}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{3}}}\right\}}}
, and showing that

R\subseteq\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}
.
∎

Lemma 34
.

For any timed local type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
if and
only if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
.

Proof.

By
Def.

6
. ∎

Lemma 35
.

For any closed, well-guarded timed local type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
,

1.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
; and

2.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}
.

Proof.

1.

By
[Sub-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu}
R]
if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}
. Otherwise,
by reflexivity (
Lemma

32
).

2.

By
[Sub-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu}
L]
if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}}
. Otherwise,
by reflexivity (
Lemma

32
).

∎

Lemma 36
.

Given a collection of mergable timed local types
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
(
i\in I
).
For all
j\in I
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{j}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}}
holds.

Proof.

By constructing a relation

R=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}})}\,\middle|\,{j\in I}\right\}}}
, and showing that

R\subseteq\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}
.
∎

Lemma 37
.

Given a collection of mergable timed local types
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
(
i\in I
).
If for all
i\in I
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
for some timed local type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
,
then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
.

Proof.

By constructing a relation

R=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T})\right\}}}
, and showing that

R\subseteq\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}
.
∎

Lemma 38
.

Given a collection of mergable timed local types
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
(
i\in I
).
If for all
i\in I
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
for some timed local type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
,
then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}}
.

Proof.

By constructing a relation

R=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}})\right\}}}
, and showing that

R\subseteq\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}
.
∎

Lemma 39
.

Given two collections of mergable timed local types
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
(
i\in I
).
If for all
i\in I
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U_{i}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}}
.

Proof.

By constructing a relation

R=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U_{i}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}})\right\}}}
, and showing that

R\subseteq\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}
.
∎

Lemma 40

(Inversion of Subtyping)
.

1.

If

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}_{i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda_{i}}\}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}=\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}^{\prime}_{j}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{j}}})}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta^{\prime}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda^{\prime}_{j}}\}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}\right\}_{j\in J}}
,
I\subseteq J
,
and
\forall k\in I:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}_{k}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}^{\prime}_{k}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{k}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{k}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta^{\prime}_{k}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta_{k}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda_{k}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{k}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{k}}
.

2.

If

\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}_{i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda_{i}}\}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}=\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}^{\prime}_{j}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{j}}})}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta^{\prime}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda^{\prime}_{j}}\}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}\right\}_{j\in J}}
,
J\subseteq I
,
and
\forall k\in J:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}_{k}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}^{\prime}_{k}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{k}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{k}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta^{\prime}_{k}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta_{k}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda_{k}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{k}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{k}}
.

3.

If

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}_{i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda_{i}}\}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}=\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}^{\prime}_{j}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{j}}})}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta^{\prime}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda^{\prime}_{j}}\}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}\right\}_{j\in J}}
,
J\subseteq I
,
and
\forall k\in J:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}_{k}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}^{\prime}_{k}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{k}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{k}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta^{\prime}_{k}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta_{k}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda_{k}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{k}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{k}}
.

4.

If

\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}_{i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda_{i}}\}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}=\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}^{\prime}_{j}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{j}}})}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta^{\prime}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda^{\prime}_{j}}\}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}\right\}_{j\in J}}
,
I\subseteq J
,
and
\forall k\in I:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}_{k}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}^{\prime}_{k}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{k}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{k}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta^{\prime}_{k}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta_{k}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda^{\prime}_{k}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda_{k}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{k}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{k}}
.

Proof.

By
Lemmas

30
and
35
, the transitivity of subtyping, and
Def.

6
(
[Sub-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&}
]
,
[Sub-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}
]
).
∎

A.3
Semantics of Timed Global Types

Lemma 41
.

\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\rangle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V^{\prime}}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}\rangle
iff

\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V}}};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}}\rangle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V^{\prime}}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}\rangle
.

Proof.

By inverting or applying
[GR-
\mu
]
when necessary.
∎

A.4
Semantics of Typing Environments

Lemma 42
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}}\right)}
.

Proof.

By induction on typing environment reductions.
∎

Lemma 43
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}
, and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}\right\}}}
, then for all

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}]}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}
with

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{subject}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}})}
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}]}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}]}}\right)}
.

Proof.

By induction on typing environment reductions.
∎

Lemma 44
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}=\mathord{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}\right\}}}
, then
for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}]}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}
with

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}\notin{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{subject}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}})}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}]}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}]}}\right)}
.

Proof.

By induction on typing context reductions.
∎

Lemma 45

(Determinism of Typing Environment Reduction)
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}
.

Proof.

By induction on typing environment reductions.
∎

Lemma 46
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
,
then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}}\right)}
.

Proof.

Directly from
Defs.

10
and
42
.
∎

Lemma 47
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
,
then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}}\right)}
.

Proof.

Directly from
Defs.

10
and

.
∎

Lemma 48
.

1.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}!}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}{:}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}_{k}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
, then for any channel with role
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}]}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\right)}
.

2.

If

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}{:}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}_{k}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
,
then for any channel with role
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}

with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}]}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}]}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}c}}\right)}
.

Proof.

By induction on typing environment reductions.
∎

A.5
Relating Semantics via Association

Lemma 49

(Relating Terminations)
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\rangle}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}}
,
then

\forall{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}]}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}\right)}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}]}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oslash}}{\mathbin{\mathbf{;\,}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\mathbin{\mathbf{,\,}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}})}}}
.

Proof.

By the definition of association (
Def.

12
), we know that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\Delta}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}
, where, by the hypothesis
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\Delta}}}\right)}=\emptyset
. Hence,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}
, which is the thesis.
∎

Lemma 50

(Inversion of Projection)
.

Given a timed local type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
, which is a supertype of projection from a timed global type

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
on a role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}
,
i.e.
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright}\,\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
,
then:

(1)

If

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}=\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}_{i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda_{i}}\}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}
,
then either

(a)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{i}}})\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{i}}\}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I^{\prime}}}
,
where
I\subseteq I^{\prime}
, and for all
i\in I:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}_{i}}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}_{i}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{i}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta_{i}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{i}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda_{i}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{i}}
,
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}{\upharpoonright}\,\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
;
or,

(b)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{t}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}_{j}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{j}}})\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{j}}\}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{j}}}}}\right\}}_{j\in J}}
, or

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}{\rightsquigarrow}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{t}}}}}{:}{k}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}_{j}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{j}}})\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{j}}\}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{j}}}}}\right\}}_{j\in J}}
,
where for all
j\in J:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{j}}}{\upharpoonright}\,\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
,
with
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{t}}}}
.

(2)

If

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}=\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}_{i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda_{i}}\}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}
, then either

(a)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{i}}})\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{i}}\}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I^{\prime}}}
, or

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}{\rightsquigarrow}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}{:}{j}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{i}}})\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{i}}\}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I^{\prime}}}
,
where
I^{\prime}\subseteq I
, and for all
i\in I^{\prime}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}_{i}}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}_{i}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta_{i}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{i}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda_{i}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{i}}
, and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}{\upharpoonright}\,\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
;
or,

(b)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{t}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}_{j}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{j}}})\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{j}}\}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{j}}}}}\right\}}_{j\in J}}
, or

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}{\rightsquigarrow}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{t}}}}}{:}{k}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}_{j}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{j}}})\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{j}}\}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{j}}}}}\right\}}_{j\in J}}
,
where for all
j\in J:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{j}}}{\upharpoonright}\,\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
,
with
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{t}}}}
.

(3)

If

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}\notin{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{roles}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}})}
.

Proof.

By the definition of timed global type projection (
Def.

7
); additionally, for cases (b) in
items (1) and (2), apply
Lemma

36
and the transitivity of subtyping.
∎

Lemma 51

(Matching Communication Under Projection)
.

If two timed local types
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U}
are supertype of an internal choice and an
external choice with matching roles, obtained via projection from a timed global
type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
, i.e.
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright}\,\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}=\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}_{i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}})}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda_{i}}\}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}

and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright}\,\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}U}}\right)}=\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}^{\prime}_{j}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{j}}})}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta^{\prime}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lambda^{\prime}_{j}}\}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}\right\}_{j\in I_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}
, then

I_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\subseteq I_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
, and

\forall i\in I_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}_{i}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}^{\prime}_{i}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{i}}
.

Proof.

By induction on items (1) and (2) of
Lemma

50
simultaneously.

(a)

We have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime\prime}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime\prime}_{i}}})\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{i}}\}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}
,

I_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\subseteq I\subseteq I_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
,

\forall i\in I_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}_{i}}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime\prime}_{i}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime\prime}_{i}}
, and

\forall i\in I:{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime\prime}_{i}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}^{\prime}_{i}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime\prime}_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{i}}
.
We have
I_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\subseteq I_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
(by transitivity of
\subseteq
),
and
\forall i\in I_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}_{i}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}^{\prime}_{i}}
(by transitivity of
=
) and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime}_{i}}
(by transitivity of

\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}
).

(b)

We have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{t}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime\prime}_{j}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime\prime}_{j}}})\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{i}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{i}}\}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{j}}}}}\right\}}_{j\in J}}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}{\rightsquigarrow}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{t}}}}}{:}{k}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime\prime}_{j}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S^{\prime\prime}_{j}}})\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{\text{O}}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta^{\prime\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{j}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{\text{I}}}_{j}}\}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{j}}}}}\right\}}_{j\in J}}

Lemma 52

(Matching Communication Under Association)
.

Let

be a typing environment containing only queue types for queries. If

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}

Lemma 55
.

Given associated timed global type
\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\rangle
and typing environment
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\rangle}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}}
.
If
\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\rangle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V^{\prime}}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}\rangle
, then there exists
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V^{\prime}}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}\rangle}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}}}
.

Proof.

By
[GR-t]
, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V^{\prime}}}
=
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V}}+t
and
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
. The thesis then holds by
Lemmas

54
and
53
.
∎

Lemma 56
.

Given associated timed global type
\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\rangle
and typing environment
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\rangle}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}}}
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
, then there exists
\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V^{\prime}}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}\rangle
such that

\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\rangle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t}}\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V^{\prime}}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}\rangle
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V^{\prime}}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}\rangle}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}}}
.

Proof.

By
Lemma

54
, we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}+t
.
The thesis then holds by
[GR-t]
and
Lemma

53
.
∎

See

Proof.

By induction on reductions of timed global type
\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\rangle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V^{\prime\prime\prime}}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime\prime\prime}}}\rangle
.
We develop several particularly interesting cases in detail.

: the thesis holds by
Lemma

.

.

Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V^{\prime}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\delta_{\text{O}}}_{k}}
, we can apply
[
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus}

Corollary 57
.

Assume that for any session
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}
, there exists a timed global
type
\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}}\rangle
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}}\rangle}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}}}
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
, then for any
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
,
there exists a timed global
type
\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V^{\prime}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}}\rangle
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V^{\prime}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}}\rangle}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}}}}}
.

By induction on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}}
.

Subtyping Properties

Proof.

From the premise, we have that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{\Delta}}\mathpunct{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9},}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}
is associated with
\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{V}}};{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\rangle
for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}
and

is
associated with
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}}\right)}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Gamma^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}}}\right)}
